[b2.182] 2 Like flames of fire his servants are, And light surrounds his dwelling place; But, O ye fiery flames, declare The brighter glories of his face.

[b2.183] 3 'Tis not for such poor worms as we To speak so infinite a thing, But your immortal eyes survey The beauties of your sovereign King.

[b2.184] 4 Tell how he shews his smiling face, And clothes all heaven in bright array; Triumph and joy run thro' the place, And songs eternal as the day.

[b2.185] 5 Speak, (for you feel his burning love) What zeal it spreads thro' all your frame: That sacred fire dwells all above, For we on earth have lost the name.

[b2.186] 6 [Sing of his power and justice too, That infinite right-hand of his That vanquish'd Satan and his crew, And thunder drove them down from bliss.

[b2.187] 7 [What mighty storms of poison'd darts Were hurl'd upon the rebels there! What deadly jav'lins nail'd their hearts Fast to the racks of long despair!]

[b2.188] 8 [Shout to your King, you heavenly host, You that beheld the sinking foe; Firmly ye stood when they were lost; Praise the rich grace that kept you so.]

[b2.189] 9 Proclaim his wonders from the skies, Let every distant nation hear; And while you sound his lofty praise, Let humble mortals bow and fear.

[b2.190] Hymn 2:28. Death and eternity.

[b2.191] 1 Stoop down, my thoughts, that use to rise, Converse awhile with death: Think how a gasping mortal lies, And pants away his breath.

[b2.192] 2 His quivering lip hangs feebly down His pulses faint and few, Then, speechless, with a doleful groan He bids the world adieu.

[b2.193] 3 But, O the soul that never dies! At once it leaves the clay! Ye thoughts, pursue it where it flies, And track its wondrous way.

[b2.194] 4 Up to the courts where angels dwell, It mounts triumphing there, Or devils plunge it down to hell In infinite despair.

[b2.195] 5 And must my body faint and die? And must this soul remove? O for some guardian angel nigh To bear it safe above!

[b2.196] 6 Jesus, to thy dear faithful hand My naked soul I trust, And my flesh waits for thy command To drop into my dust.

[b2.197] Hymn 2:29. Redemption by price and power.

[b2.198] 1 Jesus, with all thy saints above My tongue would bear her part, Would sound aloud thy saving love, And sing thy bleeding heart.

[b2.199] 2 Bless'd be the Lamb, my dearest Lord, Who bought me with his blood, And quench'd his Father's flaming sword In his own vital blood:

[b2.200] 3 The Lamb that freed my captive soul From Satan's heavy chains, And sent the lion down to howl Where hell and horror reigns.

[b2.201] 4 All glory to the dying Lamb, And never ceasing praise, While angels live to know his Name, Or saints to feel his grace.

[b2.202] Hymn 2:30. Heavenly joy on earth.

[b2.203] 1 [Come, we that love the Lord, And let our joys be known; Join in a song with sweet accord, And thus surround the throne.

[b2.204] 2 The sorrows of the mind Be banish'd from the place! Religion never was design'd To make our pleasures less.]

[b2.205] 3 Let those refuse to sing That never knew our God, But favourites of the heavenly King May speak their joys abroad.

[b2.206] 4 [The God that rules on high, And thunders when he please, That rides upon the stormy sky And manages the seas.]

[b2.207] 5 This awful God is ours, Our Father and our love, He shall send down his heavenly powers To carry us above.

[b2.208] 6 There we shall see his face, And never, never sin; There from the rivers of his grace Drink endless pleasures in.

[b2.209] 7 Yes, and before we rise To that immortal state, The thoughts of such amazing bliss Should constant joys create.

[b2.210] 8 [The men of grace have found Glory begun below, Celestial fruits on earthly ground From faith and hope may grow.]

[b2.211] 9 The hill of Sion yields A thousand sacred sweets, Before we reach the heavenly fields, Or walk the golden streets.

[b2.212] 10 Then let our songs abound, And every tear be dry; We're marching thro' Immanuel's ground To fairer worlds on high.

[b2.213] Hymn 2:31. Christ's presence makes death easy.

[b2.214] 1 Why should we start and fear to die? What timorous worms we mortals are! Death is the gate of endless joy, And yet we dread to enter there.

[b2.215] 2 The pains, the groans, and dying strife, Fright our approaching souls away; Still we shrink back again to life, Fond of our prison and our clay.

[b2.216] 3 O, if my Lord would come and meet, My soul should stretch her wings in haste, Fly fearless thro' death's iron gate, Nor feel the terrors as she pass'd.

[b2.217] 4 Jesus can make a dying bed Feel soft as downy pillows are, While on his breast I lean my head, And breathe my life out sweetly there.

[b2.218] Hymn 2:32. Frailty and Folly.

[b2.219] 1 How short and hasty is our life! How vast our souls' affairs! Yet senseless mortals vainly strive To lavish out their years.

[b2.220] 2 Our days run thoughtlessly along, Without a moment's stay; Just like a story or a song We pass our lives away.

[b2.221] 3 God from on high invites us home, But we march heedless on, And ever hastening to the tomb, Stoop downwards as we run.

[b2.222] 4 How we deserve the deepest hell That slight the joys above! What chains of vengeance should we feel That break such cords of love!

[b2.223] 5 Draw us, O God, with sovereign grace, And lift our thoughts on high, That we may end this mortal race And see salvation nigh.

[b2.224] Hymn 2:33. The blessed society in heaven.

[b2.225] 1 Raise thee, my soul, fly up, and run Thro' every heavenly street, And say, there's nought below the sun That's worthy of thy feet.

[b2.226] 2 [Thus will we mount on sacred wings, And tread the courts above; Nor earth, nor all her mightiest things Shall tempt our meanest love.]

[b2.227] 3 There on a high majestic throne Th' Almighty Father reigns, And sheds his glorious goodness down On all the blissful plains.

[b2.228] 4 Bright like a sun the Saviour sits, And spreads eternal noon, No evenings there, nor gloomy nights, To want the feeble moon.

[b2.229] 5 Amidst those ever-shining skies Behold the sacred Dove, While banish'd sin and sorrow flies From all the realms of love.

[b2.230] 6 The glorious tenants of the place Stand bending round the throne; And saints and seraphs sing and praise The infinite Three One.

[b2.231] 7 [But O what beams of heavenly grace Transport them all the while! Ten thousand smiles from Jesus' face, And love in every smile!]

[b2.232] 8 [Jesus, and when shall that dear day, That joyful hour appear, When I shall leave this house of clay To dwell amongst them there?]

[b2.233] Hymn 2:34. Breathing after the Holy Spirit; or, Fervency of devotion desired.

[b2.234] 1 Come, holy Spirit, heavenly Dove, With all thy quickening powers, Kindle a flame of sacred love, In these cold hearts of ours.

[b2.235] 2 Look, how we grovel here below, Fond of these trifling toys; Our souls can neither fly nor go To reach eternal joys.

[b2.236] 3 In vain we tune our formal songs, In vain we strive to rise; Hosannas languish on our tongues, And our devotion dies.

[b2.237] 4 Dear Lord! and shall we ever lie At this poor dying rate? Our love so faint, so cold to thee, And thine to us so great?

[b2.238] 5 Come holy Spirit, heavenly Dove, With all thy quickening powers; Come shed abroad a Saviour's love, And that shall kindle ours.

[b2.239] Hymn 2:35. Praise to God for creation and redemption.

[b2.240] 1 Let them neglect thy glory, Lord, Who never knew thy grace, But our loud songs shall still record The wonders of thy praise.

[b2.241] 2 We raise our shouts, O God, to thee, And send them to thy throne, All glory to th' united Three, The undivided One.

[b2.242] 3 'Twas he (and we'll adore his Name) That form'd us by a word, 'Tis he restores our ruin'd frame; Salvation to the Lord.

[b2.243] 4 Hosanna! let the earth and skies Repeat the joyful sound, Rocks, hills, and vales, reflect the voice In one eternal round.

[b2.244] Hymn 2:36. Christ's intercession.

[b2.245] 1 Well, the Redeemer's gone T' appear before our God, To sprinkle o'er the flaming throne With his atoning blood.

[b2.246] 2 No fiery vengeance now, Nor burning wrath comes down; If justice call for sinners' blood, The Saviour shews his own.

[b2.247] 3 Before his Father's eye Our humble suit he moves, The Father lays his thunder by, And looks, and smiles, and loves.

[b2.248] 4 Now may our joyful tongues Our Maker's honour sing, Jesus the priest receives our songs, And bears them to the King.

[b2.249] 5 [We bow before his face, And sound his glories high, "Hosanna to the God of grace "That lays his thunder by.]

[b2.250] 6 "On earth thy mercy reigns, "And triumphs all above;" But, Lord how weak are mortal strains To speak immortal love!

[b2.251] 7 [How jarring and how low Are all the notes we sing! Sweet Saviour, tune our songs anew, And they shall please the King.]

[b2.252] Hymn 2:37. The same.

[b2.253] 1 Lift up your eyes to th' heavenly seats Where your Redeemer stays; Kind intercessor, there he sits, And loves, and pleads, and prays.

[b2.254] 2 'Twas well, my soul he dy'd for thee, And shed his vital blood, Appeas'd stern justice on the tree, And then arose to God.

[b2.255] 3 Petitions now and praise may rise, And saints their offerings bring, The priest with his own sacrifice Presents them to the King.

[b2.256] 4 [Let Papists trust what names they please, Their saints and angels boast; We've no such advocates as these, Nor pray to th' heavenly host.]

[b2.257] 6 Jesus alone shall bear my cries Up to his Father's throne, He, dearest Lord! perfumes my sighs, And sweetens every groan.

[b2.258] 6 [Ten thousand praises to the King, Hosanna in the highest; Ten thousand thanks our spirits bring To God and to his Christ.]

[b2.259] Hymn 2:38. Love to God.

[b2.260] 1 Happy the heart where graces reign, Where love inspires the breast; Love is the brightest of the train, And strengthens all the rest.

[b2.261] 9 Knowledge, alas! 'Tis all in vain, And all in vain our fear, Our stubborn sins will fight and reign If love be absent there.

[b2.262] 3 'Tis love that makes our cheerful feet In swift obedience move, The devils know and tremble too, But Satan cannot love.

[b2.263] 4 This is the grace that lives and sings When faith and hope shall cease, 'Tis this shall strike our joyful strings In the sweet realms of bliss.

[b2.264] 5 Before we quite forsake our clay, Or leave this dark abode, The wings of love bear us away To see our smiling God.

[b2.265] Hymn 2:39. The shortness and misery of life.

[b2.266] 1 Our days, alas! our mortal days Are short and wretched too; "Evil and few," the patriarch says, And well the patriarch knew.

[b2.267] 2 'Tis but at best a narrow bound That heaven allows to men, And pains and sins run thro' the round Of threescore years and ten.

[b2.268] 3 Well, if ye must be sad and few, Run on, my days, in haste; Moments of sin, and months of woe, Ye cannot fly too fast.

[b2.269] 4 Let heavenly love prepare my soul, And call her to the skies, Where years of long salvation roll, And glory never dies.

[b2.270] Hymn 2:40. Our comfort in the covenant made with Christ.

[b2.271] 1 Our God, how firm his promise stands, E'en when he hides his face! He trusts in our Redeemer's hands His glory and his grace.

[b2.272] 2 Then why, my soul, these sad complaints, Since Christ and we are one; Thy God is faithful to his saints, Is faithful to his Son.

[b2.273] 3 Beneath his smiles my heart has liv'd, And part of heaven possess'd; I praise his Name for grace receiv'd, And trust him for the rest.

[b2.274] Hymn 2:41. A sight of God mortifies us to the world.

[b2.275] 1 [Up to the fields where angels lie, And living waters gently roll, Fain would my thoughts leap out and fly, But sin hangs heavy on my soul.

[b2.276] 2 Thy wondrous blood, dear dying Christ, Can make this load of guilt remove; And thou canst bear me where thou fly'st, On thy kind wings, celestial Dove!]

[b2.277] 3 O might I once mount up and see The glories of th' eternal skies, What little things these worlds would be! How despicable to my eyes!

[b2.278] 4 Had I a glance of thee, my God, Kingdoms and men would vanish soon, Vanish, as tho' I saw them not, As a dim candle dies at noon.

[b2.279] 5 Then they might fight, and rage, and rave, I should perceive the noise no more Than we can hear a shaking leaf, While rattling thunders round us roar.

[b2.280] 6 Great All in All, eternal King, Let me but view thy lovely face, And all my powers shall bow and sing Thine endless grandeur and thy grace.

[b2.281] Hymn 2:42. Delight in God.

[b2.282] 1 My God, what endless pleasures dwell Above at thy right-hand! The courts below, how amiable, Where all thy graces stand!

[b2.283] 2 The swallow near thy temple lies, And chirps a cheerful note; The lark mounts upward to thy skies, And tunes her warbling throat:

[b2.284] 3 And we, when in thy presence, Lord, We shout with joyful tongues, Or sitting round our Father's board, We crown the feast with songs.

[b2.285] 4 While Jesus shines with quickening grace, We sing and mount on high; But if a frown becloud his face, We faint, and tire, and die.

[b2.286] 5 [Just as we see the lonesome dove Bemoan her widow'd state, Wandering she flies thro' all the grove, And mourns her loving mate.

[b2.287] 6 Just so our thoughts from thing to thing In restless circles rove, Just so we drop, and hang the wing, When Jesus hides his love.]

[b2.288] Hymn 2:43. Christ's sufferings and glory.

[b2.289] 1 Now for a tune of lofty praise To great Jehovah's equal Son! Awake, my voice, in heavenly lays, Tell the loud wonders he hath done.

[b2.290] 2 Sing how he left the worlds of light And the bright robes he wore above, How swift and joyful was his flight On wings of everlasting love.

[b2.291] 3 Down to this base, this sinful earth He came to raise our nature high; He came t' atone almighty wrath; Jesus the God was born to die.]

[b2.292] 4 [Hell and its lions roar'd around, His precious blood the monsters spilt, While weighty sorrows press'd him down, Large as the loads of all our guilt.]

[b2.293] 5 Deep in the shades of gloomy death Th' almighty Captive pris'ner lay; Th' almighty Captive left the earth, And rose to everlasting day.

[b2.294] 6 Lift up your eyes, ye sons of light, Up to his throne of shining grace, See what immortal glories sit Round the sweet beauties of his face.

[b2.295] 7 Amongst a thousand harps and songs Jesus the God exalted reigns, His sacred Name fills all their tongues And echoes thro' the heavenly plains!

[b2.296] Hymn 2:44. Hell; or, The vengeance of God.

[b2.297] 1 With holy fear and humble song, The dreadful God our souls adore; Reverence and awe becomes the tongue That speaks the terrors of his power.

[b2.298] 2 Far in the deep where darkness dwells, The land of horror and despair, Justice has built a dismal hell, And laid her stores of vengeance there.

[b2.299] 3 [Eternal plagues and heavy chains, Tormenting racks and fiery coals, And darts t' inflict immortal pains, Dy'd in the blood of damned souls.]

[b2.300] 4 [There Satan the first sinner lies, And roars, and bites his iron bands; In vain the rebel strives to rise, Crush'd with the weight of both thine hands.]

[b2.301] 5 There guilty ghosts of Adam's race Shriek out, and howl beneath thy rod; Once they could scorn a Saviour's grace, But they incens'd a dreadful God.

[b2.302] 6 Tremble, my soul, and kiss the Son; Sinners, obey the Saviour's call; Else your damnation hastens on, And hell gapes wide to wait your fall.

[b2.303] Hymn 2:45. God's condescension to our worship.

[b2.304] 1 Thy favours Lord, surprise our souls; Will the Eternal dwell with us? What canst thou find beneath the poles To tempt thy chariot downward thus?

[b2.305] 2 Still might he fill his starry throne, And please his ears with Gabriel's songs; But th' heavenly Majesty comes down, And bows to hearken to our tongues.

[b2.306] 3 Great God, what poor returns we pay For love so infinite as thine! Words are but air, and tongues but clay, But thy compassion's all divine.

[b2.307] Hymn 2:46. God's condescension to human affairs.

[b2.308] 1 Up to the Lord that reigns on high, And views the nations from afar, Let everlasting praises fly, And tell how large his bounties are.

[b2.309] 2 [He that can shake the worlds he made, Or with his word, or with his rod, His goodness how amazing great! And what a condescending God!]

[b2.310] 3 [God that must stoop to view the skies, And how to see what angels do, Down to our earth he casts his eyes, And bends his footsteps downward too.]

[b2.311] 4 He over-rules all mortal things, And manages our mean affairs; On humble souls the King of kings Bestows his counsels and his cares.

[b2.312] 5 Our sorrows and our tears we pour Into the bosom of our God, He hears us in the mournful hour, And helps us bear the heavy load.

[b2.313] 6 In vain might lofty princes try Such condescension to perform; For worms were never rais'd so high Above their meanest fellow-worm.

[b2.314] 7 O could our thankful hearts devise A tribute equal to thy grace, To the third heaven our songs should rise, And teach the golden harps thy praise.

[b2.315] Hymn 2:47. Glory and grace in the person of Christ.

[b2.316] 1 Now to the Lord a noble song! Awake, my soul, awake, my tongue; Hosanna to th' eternal Name, And all his boundless love proclaim.

[b2.317] 2 See where it shines in Jesus' face, The brightest image of his grace; God, in the person of his Son, Has all his mightiest works outdone.

[b2.318] 3 The spacious earth and spreading flood Proclaim the wise, the powerful God; And thy rich glories from afar Sparkle in every rolling star.

[b2.319] 4 But in his looks a glory stands, The noblest labour of thine hands: The pleasing lustre of his eyes Outshines the wonders of the skies.

[b2.320] 5 Grace, 'tis a sweet, a charming theme; My thoughts rejoice at Jesus' name: Ye angels, dwell upon the sound, Ye heavens, reflect it to the ground!

[b2.321] 6 O, may I live to reach the place Where he unveils his lovely face, Where all his beauties you behold, And sing his Name to harps of gold!

[b2.322] Hymn 2:48. Love to the creatures is dangerous.

[b2.323] 1 How vain are all things here below! How false, and yet how fair! Each pleasure hath its poison too, And every sweet a snare.

[b2.324] 2 The brightest things below the sky Give but a flattering light; We should suspect some danger nigh Where we possess delight.

[b2.325] 3 Our dearest joys, and nearest friends, The partners of our blood, How they divide our wavering minds, And leave but half for God!

[b2.326] 4 The fondness of a creature's love, How strong it strikes the sense! Thither the warm affections move, Nor can we call them thence.

[b2.327] 5 Dear Saviour, let thy beauties be My soul's eternal food; And grace command my heart away From all created good.

[b2.328] Hymn 2:49. Moses dying in the embraces of God.

[b2.329] 1 Death cannot make our souls afraid If God be with us there; We may walk thro' her darkest shade, And never yield to fear.

[b2.330] 2 I could renounce my all below, If my Creator bid, And run, if I were call'd to go, And die as Moses did.

[b2.331] 3 Might I but climb to Pisgah's top, And view the promis'd land, My flesh itself shall long to drop, And pray for the command.

[b2.332] 4 Clasp'd in my heavenly Father's arms I would forget my breath, And lose my life among the charms Of so divine a death.

[b2.333] Hymn 2:50. Comfort under sorrows and pains.

[b2.334] 1 Now let the Lord my Saviour smile, And shew my name upon his heart, I would forget my pains awhile, And in the pleasure lose the smart.

[b2.335] But O it swells my sorrows high To see my blessed Jesus frown! My spirits sink, my comforts die, And all the springs of life are down.

[b2.336] 3 Yet why, my soul, why these complaints? Still while he frowns his bowels move; Still on his heart he bears his saints, And feels their sorrows and his love.

[b2.337] 4 My name is printed on his breast; His book of life contains my name; I'd rather have it there impress'd Than in the bright records of fame.

[b2.338] 5 When the last fire burns all things here, Those letters shall securely stand, And in the Lamb's fair book appear, Writ by th' eternal Father's hand.

[b2.339] 6 Now shall my minutes smoothly run, Whil'st here I wait my Father's will; My rising and my setting sun Roll gently up and down the hill.

[b2.340] Hymn 2:51. God the Son equal with the Father.

[b2.341] 1 Bright King of Glory, dreadful God! Our spirits bow before thy seat, To thee we lift an humble thought, And worship at thine awful feet.

[b2.342] 2 [Thy power hath form'd, thy wisdom sways All nature with a sovereign word; And the bright world of stars obeys The will of their superior Lord.]

[b2.343] 3 [Mercy and truth unite in one, And smiling sit at thy right-hand; Eternal justice guards thy throne, And vengeance waits thy dread command.]

[b2.344] 4 A thousand seraphs strong and bright Stand round the glorious Deity; But who amongst the sons of light Pretends comparison with thee?

[b2.345] 5 Yet there is one of human frame, Jesus, array'd in flesh and blood, Thinks it no robbery to claim A full equality with God.

[b2.346] 6 Their glory shines with equal beams; Their essence is for ever one, Tho' they are known by different names The Father God, and God the Son.

[b2.347] 7 Then let the name of Christ our King With equal honours be ador'd; His praise let every angel sing, And all the nations own their Lord.

[b2.348] Hymn 2:52. Death dreadful or delightful.

[b2.349] 1 Death! 'tis a melancholy day To those that have no God, When the poor soul is forc'd away To seek her last abode.

[b2.350] 2 In vain to heaven she lifts her eyes, But guilt, a heavy chain, Still drags her downward from the skies To darkness, fire, and pain.

[b2.351] 3 Awake and mourn, ye heirs of hell, Let stubborn sinners fear, You must be driven from earth, and dwell A long for-ever there.

[b2.352] 4 See how the pit gapes wide for you, And flashes in your face, And thou, my soul, look downwards too, And sing recovering grace.

[b2.353] 5 He is a God of sovereign love That promis'd heaven to me, And taught my thoughts to soar above, Where happy spirits be.

[b2.354] 6 Prepare me, Lord, for thy right-hand, Then come the joyful day, Come death, and some celestial band, To bear my soul away.

[b2.355] Hymn 2:53. The pilgrimage of the saints; or, Earth and heaven.

[b2.356] 1 Lord! what a wretched land is this That yields us no supply! No cheering fruits no wholesome trees, Nor streams of living joy!

[b2.357] 2 But pricking thorns thro' all the ground And mortal poisons grow, And all the rivers that are found With dangerous waters flow.

[b2.358] 3 Yet the clear path to thine abode Lies thro' this horrid land; Lord! we would keep the heavenly road, And run at thy command.

[b2.359] 4 [Our souls shall tread the desert thro' With undiverted feet; And faith and flaming zeal subdue The terrors that we meet.]

[b2.360] 5 [A thousand savage beasts of prey Around the forest roam; But Judah's lion guards the way, And guides the strangers home.]

[b2.361] 6 [Long nights and darkness dwell below, With scarce a twinkling ray; But the bright world to which we go Is everlasting day.]

[b2.362] 7 [By glimmering hopes and gloomy fears We trace the sacred road, Thro' dismal deeps and dangerous snares We make our way to God.]

[b2.363] 8 Our journey is a thorny maze, But we march upward still; Forget these troubles of the ways, And reach at Zion's hill.

[b2.364] 9 [See the kind angels at the gates, Inviting us to come; There Jesus the fore-runner waits To welcome travellers home.]

[b2.365] 10 There on a green and flowery mount Our weary souls shall sit, And with transporting joys recount The labours of our feet.

[b2.366] 11 [No vain discourse shall fill our tongue, Nor trifles vex our ear, Infinite grace shall be our song, And God rejoice to hear.]

[b2.367] 12 Eternal glories to the King That brought us safely thro'; Our tongues shall never cease to sing, And endless praise renew.

[b2.368] Hymn 2:54. God's presence is light in darkness.

[b2.369] 1 My God, the spring of all my joys, The life of my delights, The glory of my brightest days, And comfort of my nights.

[b2.370] 2 In darkest shades if he appear, My dawning is begun; He is my soul's sweet morning star And he my rising sun.

[b2.371] 3 The opening heavens around me shine With beams of sacred bliss, While Jesus shews his heart is mine, And whispers, "I am his!"

[b2.372] 4 My soul would leave this heavy clay At that transporting word, Run up with joy the shining way T' embrace my dearest Lord.

[b2.373] 5 Fearless of hell and ghastly death! I'd break thro' every foe; The wings of love, and arms of faith Should bear me conqueror thro'.

[b2.374] Hymn 2:55. Frail life and succeeding eternity.

[b2.375] 1 Thee we adore, eternal Name, And humbly own to thee, How feeble is our mortal frame! What dying worms are we!

[b2.376] 2 [Our wasting lives grow shorter still As months and days increase; And every beating pulse we tell Leaves but the number less.

[b2.377] 3 The year rolls round, and steals away The breath that first it gave; Whate'er we do, where'er we be, We're travelling to the grave.

[b2.378] 4 Dangers stand thick thro' all the ground To push us to the tomb, And fierce diseases wait around To hurry mortals home.

[b2.379] 5 Good God! on what a slender thread Hang everlasting things! Th' eternal states of all the dead Upon life's feeble strings.

[b2.380] 6 Infinite joy or endless woe Attends on every breath; And yet how unconcern'd we go Upon the brink of death!

[b2.381] 7 Waken, O Lord, our drowsy sense To walk this dangerous road; And if our souls are hurried hence, May they be found with God!

[b2.382] Hymn 2:56. The misery of being without God in this world; or, Vain prosperity.

[b2.383] 1 O, I shall envy them no more Who grow profanely great, Tho' they increase their golden store, And rise to wondrous height.

[b2.384] 2 They taste of all the joys that grow Upon this earthly clod, Well they may search the creature thro', For they have ne'er a God.

[b2.385] 3 Shake off the thoughts of dying too, And think your life your own; But death comes hastening on to you To mow your glory down.

[b2.386] 4 Yes, you must bow your stately head, Away your spirit flies, And no kind angel near your bed To bear it to the skies.

[b2.387] 5 Go now, and boast of all your stores, And tell how bright you shine; Your heaps of glittering dust are yours, And my Redeemer's mine.

[b2.388] Hymn 2:57. The pleasures of a good conscience.

[b2.389] 1 Lord, how secure and bless'd are they Who feel the joys of pardon'd sin! Should storms of wrath shake earth and sea, Their minds have heaven and peace within.

[b2.390] 2 The day glides sweetly o'er their heads, Made up of innocence and love; And soft and silent as the shades Their nightly minutes gently move.

[b2.391] 3 [Quick as their thoughts their joys come on, But fly not half so fast away; Their souls are ever bright as noon, And calm as summer evenings be.

[b2.392] 4 How oft they look to th' heavenly hills, Where groves of living pleasure grow! And longing hopes and cheerful smiles Sit undisturb'd upon their brow.]

[b2.393] 5 They scorn to seek our golden toys, But spend the day and share the night In numbering o'er the richer joys That heaven prepares for their delight.

[b2.394] 6 While wretched we, like worms and moles, Lie grovelling in the dust below: Almighty grace, renew our souls, And we'll aspire to glory too.

[b2.395] Hymn 2:58 The shortness of life, and the goodness of God.

[b2.396] 1 Time! what an empty vapour 'tis! And days how swift they are! Swift as an Indian arrow flies, Or like a shooting star.

[b2.397] 2 [The present moments just appear, Then slide away in haste, That we can never say, "They're here," But only say, "They're past."]

[b2.398] 3 [Our life is ever on the wing, And death is ever nigh; The moment when our lives begin We all begin to die.]

[b2.399] 4 Yet, mighty God, our fleeting days Thy lasting favours share, Yet with the bounties of thy grace Thou load'st the rolling year.

[b2.400] 5 'Tis sovereign mercy finds us food, And we are cloth d with love; While grace stands pointing out the road That leads our souls above.

[b2.401] 6 His goodness runs an endless round; All glory to the Lord: His mercy never knows a bound, And be his Name ador'd!

[b2.402] 7 Thus we begin the lasting song, And when we close our eyes, Let the next age thy praise prolong Till time and nature dies.

[b2.403] Hymn 2:59. Paradise on earth.

[b2.404] 1 Glory to God that walks the sky, And sends his blessings thro', That tells his saints of joys on high, And gives a taste below.

[b2.405] 2 [Glory to God that stoops his throne That dust and worms may see't, And brings a glimpse of glory down Around his sacred feet.

[b2.406] 3 When Christ, with all his graces crown'd, Sheds his kind beams abroad, 'Tis a young heaven on earthly ground, And glory in the bud.

[b2.407] 4 A blooming paradise of joy In this wild desert springs; And every sense I straight employ On sweet celestial things.

[b2.408] 5 White lilies all around appear, And each his glory shows; The rose of Sharon blossoms here, The fairest flower that blows.

[b2.409] 6 Cheerful I feast on heavenly fruit, And drink the pleasures down, Pleasures that flow hard by the foot Of the eternal throne.]

[b2.410] 7 But ah! how soon my joys decay, How soon my sins arise, And snatch the heavenly scene away From these lamenting eyes!

[b2.411] 8 When shall the time, dear Jesus, when The shining day appear, That I shall leave those clouds of sin, And guilt and darkness here?

[b2.412] 9 Up to the fields above the skies My hasty feet would go, There everlasting flowers arise, And joys unwithering grow.

[b2.413] Hymn 2:60. The truth of God the promiser; or, The promises are our security.

[b2.414] 1 Praise, everlasting praise be paid To him that earth's foundations laid; Praise to the God whose strong decrees Sway the creation as he please.

[b2.415] 2 Praise to the goodness of the Lord Who rules his people by his word, And there as strong as his decrees He sets his kindest promises.

[b2.416] 3 [Firm are the words his prophets give, Sweet words on which his children live; Each of them is the voice of God, Who spoke and spread the skies abroad.

[b2.417] 4 Each of them powerful as that sound That bid the new-made heavens go round; And stronger than the solid poles, On which the wheel of nature rolls.]

[b2.418] 5 Whence then should doubts and fears arise, Why trickling sorrows drown our eyes? Slowly, alas, our mind receives The comforts that our Maker gives.

[b2.419] 6 O for a strong, a lasting faith To credit what th' almighty saith! T' embrace the message of his Son, And call the joys of heaven our own.

[b2.420] 7 Then should the earth's old pillars shake, And all the wheels of nature break, Our steady souls should fear no more Than solid rocks when billows roar.

[b2.421] 8 Our everlasting hopes arise Above the ruinable skies, Where the eternal Builder reigns, And his own courts his power sustains.

[b2.422] Hymn 2:61. A thought of death and glory.

[b2.423] 1 My soul, come meditate the day, And think how near it stands, When thou must quit this house of clay, And fly to unknown lands.

[b2.424] 2 [And you, mine eyes, look down and view The hollow gaping tomb, This gloomy prison waits for you Whene'er the summons come.]

[b2.425] 3 O could we die with those that die, And place us in their stead, Then would our spirits learn to fly, And converse with the dead:

[b2.426] 4 Then should we see the saints above In their own glorious forms, And wonder why our souls should love To dwell with mortal worms.

[b2.427] 5 [How we should scorn these clothes of flesh, These fetters and this load! And long for evening to undress, That we may rest with God.]

[b2.428] 6 We should almost forsake our clay Before the summons come, And pray, and wish our souls away To their eternal home.

[b2.429] Hymn 2:62. God the thunderer; or, The last judgment and hell.*

[b2.430] 1 Sing to the Lord, ye heavenly hosts, And thou, O earth, adore, Let death and hell thro' all their coasts, Stand trembling at his power.

[b2.431] 2 His sounding chariot shakes the sky, He makes the clouds his throne, There all his stores of lightning lie, Till vengeance dart them down.

[b2.432] 3 His nostrils breathe out fiery streams, And from his awful tongue A sovereign voice divides the flames, And thunder roars along.

[b2.433] 4 Think, O my soul, the dreadful day When this incensed God Shall rend the sky, and burn the sea, And fling his wrath abroad.

[b2.434] 5 What shall the wretch the sinner do? He once defy'd the Lord; But he shall dread the Thunderer now, And sink beneath his word.

[b2.435] 6 Tempests of angry fire shall roll To blast the rebel-worm, And beat upon his naked soul In one eternal storm.

[b2.436] * Made in a great sudden storm of thunder, August 20, 1697.

[b2.437] Hymn 2:63. A funeral thought.

[b2.438] 1 Hark! from the tombs a doleful sound, My ears attend the cry, "Ye living men, come view the ground "Where you must shortly lie.

[b2.439] 2 "Princes, this clay must be your bed, "In spite of all your towers; "The tall, the wise, the reverend head "Must lie as low as ours."

[b2.440] 3 Great God, is this our certain doom? And are we still secure? Still walking downward to our tomb, And yet prepare no more?

[b2.441] 4 Grant us the powers of quickening grace To fit our souls to fly, Then, when we drop this dying flesh, We'll rise above the sky.

[b2.442] Hymn 2:64. God the glory and defence of Sion.

[b2.443] 1 Happy the church, thou sacred place, The seat of thy Creator's grace; Thine holy courts are his abode, Thou earthly palace of our God.

[b2.444] 2 Thy walls are strength, and at thy gates A guard of heavenly warriors waits; Nor shall thy deep foundations move, Fix'd on his counsels and his love.

[b2.445] 3 Thy foes in vain designs engage, Against his throne in vain they rage, Like rising waves, with angry roar, That dash and die upon the shore.

[b2.446] 4 Then let our souls in Zion dwell, Nor fear the wrath of Rome and hell: His arms embrace this happy ground, Like brazen bulwarks built around.

[b2.447] 5 God is our shield, and God our sun; Swift as the fleeting moments run, On us he sheds new beams of grace, And we reflect his brightest praise.

[b2.448] Hymn 2:65. The hope of heaven our support under trials on earth.

[b2.449] 1 When I can read my title clear To mansions in the skies, I bid farewell to every fear, And wipe my weeping eyes.

[b2.450] 2 Should earth against my soul engage, And hellish darts be hurl'd, Then I can smile at Satan's rage, And face a frowning world.

[b2.451] 3 Let cares like a wild deluge come, And storms of sorrow fall, May I but safely reach my home, My God, my heaven, my all.

[b2.452] 4 There shall I bathe my weary soul In seas of heavenly rest, And not a wave of trouble roll Across my peaceful breast.

[b2.453] Hymn 2:66. A prospect of heaven makes death easy.

[b2.454] 1 There is a land of pure delight Where saints immortal reign, Infinite day excludes the night, And pleasures banish pain.

[b2.455] 2 There everlasting spring abides, And never withering flowers: Death like a narrow sea divides This heavenly land from ours.

[b2.456] 3 [Sweet fields beyond the swelling flood, Stand dress'd in living green So to the Jews old Canaan stood, While Jordan roll'd between.

[b2.457] 4 But timorous mortals start and shrink To cross this narrow sea, And linger shivering on the brink, And fear to launch away.]

[b2.458] 5 O! could we make our doubts remove, These gloomy doubts that rise, And see the Canaan that we love, With unbeclouded eyes!

[b2.459] 6 Could we but climb where Moses stood, And view the landscape o'er, Not Jordan's stream, nor death's cold flood, Should fright us from the shore.

[b2.460] Hymn 2:67. God's eternal dominion.

[b2.461] 1 Great God, how infinite art thou! What worthless worms are we! Let the whole race of creatures bow And pay their praise to thee.

[b2.462] 2 Thy throne eternal ages stood, Ere seas or stars were made; Thou art the ever-living God Were all the nations dead.

[b2.463] 3 Nature and time quite naked lie To thine immense survey, From the formation of the sky To the great burning day.

[b2.464] 4 Eternity with all its years Stands present in thy view; To thee there's nothing old appears, Great God, there's nothing new.

[b2.465] 5 Our lives thro' various scenes are drawn, And vex'd with trifling cares; While thine eternal thought moves on Thine undisturb'd affairs.

[b2.466] 6 Great God, how infinite art thou! What worthless worms are we! Let the whole race of creatures bow And pay their praise to thee.

[b2.467] Hymn 2:68. The humble worship of heaven.

[b2.468] 1 Father, I long, I faint to see The place of thine abode, I'd leave thy earthly courts and flee Up to thy seat, my God!

[b2.469] 2 Here I behold thy distant face, And 'tis a pleasing sight; But to abide in thine embrace Is infinite delight.

[b2.470] 3 I'd part with all the joys of sense To gaze upon thy throne; Pleasure springs fresh for ever thence, Unspeakable, unknown.

[b2.471] 4 [There all the heavenly hosts are seen, In shining ranks they move, And drink immortal vigour in, With wonder and with love.

[b2.472] 5 Then at thy feet with awful fear Th' adoring armies fall With joy they shrink to nothing there, Before th' Eternal All.

[b2.473] 6 There I would vie with all the host In duty and in bliss, While less than nothing I could boast, And vanity confess.] __

[b2.474] 7 The more thy glories strike mine eyes, The humbler I shall lie; Thus while I sink, my joys shall rise Unmeasurably high.

[b2.475] __ Isaiah 40:17.

[b2.476] Hymn 2:69. The faithfulness of God in his promises.

[b2.477] 1 [Begin, my tongue, some heavenly theme, And speak some boundless thing, The mighty works, or mightier name Of our eternal King.

[b2.478] 2 Tell of his wondrous faithfulness, And sound his power abroad, Sing the sweet promise of his grace, And the performing God.

[b2.479] 3 Proclaim "salvation from the Lord "For wretched dying men;" His hand has writ the sacred word With an immortal pen.

[b2.480] 4 Engrav'd as in eternal brass, The mighty promise shines; Nor can the powers of darkness rase Those everlasting lines.]

[b2.481] 5 [He that can dash whole worlds to death And make them when he please, He speaks, and that almighty breath Fulfils his great decrees.

[b2.482] 6 His very word of grace is strong As that which built the skies, The voice that rolls the stars along Speaks all the promises.

[b2.483] 7 He said, "Let the wide heaven be spread," And heaven was stretch'd abroad; "Abrah'm, I'll be thy God," he said, And he was Abrah'm's God.

[b2.484] 8 O, might I hear thine heavenly tongue But whisper, "Thou art mine;" Those gentle words should raise my song To notes almost divine.

[b2.485] 9 How would my leaping heart rejoice And think my heaven secure! I trust the all-creating voice, And faith desires no more.]

[b2.486] Hymn 2:70. God's dominion over the sea, Psalm 107. 23 &c.

[b2.487] 1 God of the seas, thy thundering voice Makes all the roaring waves rejoice, And one soft word of thy command Can sink them silent in the sand.

[b2.488] 2 If but a Moses wave thy rod, The sea divides, and owns its God: The stormy floods their Maker knew, And let his chosen armies thro'.

[b2.489] 3 The scaly flocks amidst the sea, To thee, their Lord, a tribute pay; The meanest fish that swims the flood Leaps up, and means a praise to God.

[b2.490] 4 [The larger monsters of the deep, On thy commands attendance keep, By thy permission sport and play, And cleave along their foaming way.

[b2.491] 5 If God his voice of tempest rears, Leviathan lies still and fears, Anon he lifts his nostrils high, And spouts the ocean to the sky.]

[b2.492] 6 How is thy glorious power ador'd, Amidst those watery nations, Lord! Yet the bold men that trace the seas, Bold men, refuse their Maker's praise.

[b2.493] 7 [What scenes of miracle they see, And never tune a song to thee! While on the flood they safely ride, They curse the hand that smooths the tide.

[b2.494] 8 Anon they plunge in watery graves, And some drink death among the waves: Yet the surviving crew blaspheme, Nor own the God that rescu'd them.]

[b2.495] 9 O for some signal of thine hand! Shake all the seas, Lord, shake the land, Great Judge, descend, lest men deny That there's a God that rules the sky.

[b2.496] From the 70th to the 109th Hymn, I hope the reader will forgive the neglect of the rhyme in the first and third lines of the stanza.

[b2.497] Hymn 2:71. Praise to God from all creatures.

[b2.498] 1 The glories of my Maker God, My joyful voice shall sing, And call the nations to adore Their Former and their King.

[b2.499] 2 'Twas his right-hand that shap'd our clay, And wrought this human frame, But from his own immediate breath Our nobler spirits came.

[b2.500] 3 We bring our mortal powers to God, And worship with our tongues: We claim some kindred with the skies And join th' angelic songs.

[b2.501] 4 Let groveling beasts of every shape, And fowls of every wing, And rocks, and trees, and fires, and seas, Their various tribute bring.

[b2.502] 5 Ye planets, to his honour shine, And wheels of nature roll, Praise him in your unwearied course Around the steady pole.

[b2.503] 6 The brightness of our Maker's Name The wide creation fills, And his unbounded grandeur flies Beyond the heavenly hills.

[b2.504] Hymn 2:72. The Lord's Day; or, The resurrection of Christ.

[b2.505] 1 Bless'd morning, whose young dawning rays Beheld our rising God, That saw him triumph o'er the dust, And leave his dark abode.

[b2.506] 2 In the cold prison of a tomb, The dead Redeemer lay, Till the revolving skies had brought The third, th' appointed day.

[b2.507] 3 Hell and the grave unite their force To hold our God in vain, The sleeping Conqueror arose, And burst their feeble chain.

[b2.508] 4 To thy great Name, Almighty Lord, These sacred hours we pay, And loud hosannas shall proclaim The triumph of the day,

[b2.509] 5 [Salvation and immortal praise To our victorious King, Let heaven, and earth, and rocks, and seas, With glad hosannas ring.]

[b2.510] Hymn 2:73. Doubts scattered; or, Spiritual joy restored.

[b2.511] 1 Hence from my soul, sad thoughts, be gone, And leave me to my joys, My tongue shall triumph in my God, And make a joyful noise.

[b2.512] 2 Darkness and doubts had veil'd my mind, And drown'd my head in tears, Till sovereign grace with shining rays Dispell'd my gloomy fears.

[b2.513] 3 O what immortal joys I felt, And raptures all divine, When Jesus told me, I was his, And my Beloved mine.

[b2.514] 4 In vain the tempter frights my soul, And breaks my peace in vain, One glimpse, dear Saviour, of thy face, Revives my joys again.

[b2.515] Hymn 2:74. Repentance from a sense of divine goodness; or, A complaint of ingratitude.

[b2.516] 1 Is this the kind return, And these the thanks we owe, Thus to abuse eternal love, Whence all our blessings flow?

[b2.517] 2 TO what a stubborn frame Has sin reduc'd our mind! What strange rebellious wretches we, And God as strangely kind!

[b2.518] 3 [On us he bids the sun Shed his reviving rays, For us the skies their circles run To lengthen out our days.

[b2.519] 4 The brutes obey their God, And bow their necks to men, But we more base, more brutish things Reject his easy reign.]

[b2.520] 5 Turn, turn us, mighty God, And mould our souls afresh, Break, sovereign grace, these hearts of stone, And give us hearts of flesh.

[b2.521] 6 Let old ingratitude Provoke our weeping eyes, And hourly as new mercies fall Let hourly thanks arise.

[b2.522] Hymn 2:75. Spiritual and eternal joys; or, The beatific sight of Christ.

[b2.523] 1 From thee, my God, my joys shall rise, And run eternal rounds, Beyond the limits of the skies And all created bounds.

[b2.524] 2 The holy triumphs of my soul Shall death itself out-brave, Leave dull mortality behind, And fly beyond the grave.

[b2.525] 3 There, where my blessed Jesus reigns In heaven's unmeasur'd space, I'll spend a long eternity In pleasure and in praise.

[b2.526] 4 Millions of years my wondering eyes Shall o'er thy beauties rove, And endless ages I'll adore The glories of thy love.

[b2.527] 5 [Sweet Jesus, every smile of thine Shall fresh endearments bring, And thousand tastes of new delight From all thy graces spring.

[b2.528] 6 Haste, my beloved, fetch my soul Up to thy bless'd abode, Fly, for my spirit longs to see My Saviour and my God.

[b2.529] Hymn 2:76. The resurrection and ascension of Christ.

[b2.530] 1 Hosanna to the Prince of Light, That cloth'd himself in clay, Enter'd the iron gates of death, And tore the bars away.

[b2.531] 2 Death is no more the king of dread Since our Immanuel rose, He took the tyrant's sting away, And spoil'd our hellish foes.

[b2.532] 3 See how the Conqueror mounts aloft, And to his Father flies, With scars of honour in his flesh, And triumph in his eyes.

[b2.533] 4 There our exalted Saviour reigns, And scatters blessings down, Our Jesus fills the middle seat Of the celestial throne.

[b2.534] 5 [Raise your devotion, mortal tongues, To reach his bless'd abode, Sweet be the accents of your songs To our incarnate God.

[b2.535] 6 Bright angels, strike your loudest strings, Your sweetest voices raise, Let heaven, and all created things, Sound our Immanuel's praise.]

[b2.536] Hymn 77. The Christian warfare.

[b2.537] 1 [Stand up, my soul, shake off thy fears, And gird the gospel-armour on, March to the gates of endless joy Where thy great Captain-Saviour's gone.

[b2.538] 2 Hell and thy sins resist thy course, But hell and sin are vanquish'd foes, Thy Jesus nail'd them to the cross, And sung the triumph when he rose.]

[b2.539] 3 [What tho' the prince of darkness rage, And waste the fury of his spite, Eternal chains confine him down To fiery deeps, and endless night.

[b2.540] 4 What tho' thine inward lusts rebel, 'Tis but a struggling gasp for life The weapons of victorious grace Shall slay thy sins, and end the strife.]

[b2.541] 5 Then let my soul march boldly on, Press forward to the heavenly gate, There peace and joy eternal reign, And glittering robes for conquerors wait.

[b2.542] 6 There shall I wear a starry crown, And triumph in almighty grace, While all the armies of the skies Join in my glorious leader's praise.

[b2.543] Hymn 2:78. Redemption by Christ.

[b2.544] 1 When the first parents of our race Rebell'd and lost their God, And the infection of their sin Had tainted all our blood;

[b2.545] 2 Infinite pity touch'd the heart Of the eternal Son, Descending from the heavenly court He left his Father's throne.

[b2.546] 3 Aside the Prince of glory threw His most divine array, And wrapt his Godhead in a veil Of our inferior clay.

[b2.547] 4 His living power, and dying love Redeem'd unhappy men, And rais'd the ruins of our race To life and God again.

[b2.548] 5 To thee, dear Lord, our flesh and soul We joyfully resign, Bless'd Jesus, take us for thy own, For we are doubly thine.

[b2.549] 6 Thine honour shall for ever be The business of our days, For ever shall our thankful tongue Speak thy deserved praise.

[b2.550] Hymn 2:79. Praise to the Redeemer.

[b2.551] 1 Plung'd in a gulph of dark despair We wretched sinners lay, Without one cheerful beam of hope, Or spark of glimmering day.

[b2.552] 2 With pitying eyes, the Prince of Grace Beheld our helpless grief, He saw, and (O amazing love!) He ran to our relief.

[b2.553] 3 Down from the shining seats above With joyful haste he fled, Enter'd the grave in mortal flesh, And dwelt among the dead.

[b2.554] 4 He spoil'd the Powers of darkness thus, And brake our iron chains; Jesus has freed our captive souls From everlasting pains.

[b2.555] 5 [In vain the baffled prince of hell His cursed projects tries, We that were doom'd his endless slaves, Are rais'd above the skies.]

[b2.556] 6 O for this love, let rocks and hills Their lasting silence break, And all harmonious human tongues The Saviour's praises speak.

[b2.557] 7 [Yes, we will praise thee, dearest Lord, Our souls are all on flame, Hosanna round the spacious earth To thine adored Name.

[b2.558] 8 Angels, assist our mighty joys, Strike all your harps of gold; But when you raise your highest notes His love can ne'er be told.]

[b2.559] Hymn 2:80. God's awful power and goodness.

[b2.560] 1 O the Almighty Lord! How matchless is his power! Tremble, O earth, beneath his word, And all the heavens adore.

[b2.561] 2 Let proud imperious kings Bow low before his throne, Crouch to his feet, ye haughty things, Or he shall tread you down.

[b2.562] 3 Above the skies he reigns, And with amazing blows He deals unsufferable pains On his rebellious foes.

[b2.563] 4 Yet, everlasting God, We love to speak thy praise; Thy sceptre's equal to thy rod, The sceptre of thy grace.

[b2.564] 5 The arms of mighty love Defend our Sion well, And heavenly mercy walls us round From Babylon and hell.

[b2.565] 6 Salvation to the King That sits enthron'd above; Thus we adore the God of might, And bless the God of love.

[b2.566] Hymn 2:81. Our sin the cause of Christ's death.

[b2.567] 1 And now the scales have left mine eyes, Now I begin to see; O the curs'd deeds my sins have done! What murderous things they be!

[b2.568] 2 Were these the traitors, dearest Lord, That thy fair body tore? Monsters, that stain'd those heavenly limbs With floods of purple gore?

[b2.569] 3 Was it for crimes that I had done My dearest Lord was slain When justice seiz'd God's only Son, And put his soul to pain?

[b2.570] 4 Forgive my guilt, O Prince of peace, I'll wound my God no more; Hence from my heart, ye sins, be gone, For Jesus I adore.

[b2.571] 5 Furnish me, Lord, with heavenly arms From grace's magazine, And I'll proclaim eternal war With every darling sin.

[b2.572] Hymn 2:82. Redemption and protection from spiritual enemies.

[b2.573] 1 Arise my soul, my joyful powers, And triumph in my God, Awake, my voice, and loud proclaim His glorious grace abroad.

[b2.574] 2 He rais'd me from the deeps of sin, The gates of gaping hell, And fix'd my standing more secure Than 'twas before I fell.

[b2.575] 3 The arms of everlasting love Beneath my soul he plac'd, And on the rock of ages set My slippery footsteps fast.

[b2.576] 4 The city of my bless'd abode Is wall'd around with grace Salvation for a bulwark stands To shield the sacred place.

[b2.577] 5 Satan may vent his sharpest spite, And all his legions roar, Almighty mercy guards my life, And bounds his raging power.

[b2.578] 6 Arise, my soul, awake, my voice, And tunes of pleasure sing, Loud hallelujahs shall address My Saviour and my King.

[b2.579] Hymn 2:83. The passion and exaltation of Christ.

[b2.580] 1 Thus saith the Ruler of the skies, "Awake, my dreadful sword; "Awake, my wrath and smite the man, "My fellow," saith the Lord.

[b2.581] 2 Vengeance receiv'd the dread command, And armed down she flies, Jesus submits t' his Father's hand, And bows his head and dies.

[b2.582] 3 But O! the wisdom and the grace That join with vengeance now! He dies to save our guilty race, And yet he rises too.

[b2.583] 4 A person so divine was he Who yielded to be slain, That he could give his soul away, And take his life again.

[b2.584] 5 Live, glorious Lord, and reign on high, Let every nation sing, And angels sound with endless joy The Saviour and the King.

[b2.585] Hymn 2:84. The same.

[b2.586] 1 Come, all harmonious tongues, Your noblest music bring, 'Tis Christ the everlasting God, And Christ the man we sing.

[b2.587] 2 Tell how he took our flesh To take away our guilt, Sing the dear drops of sacred blood That hellish monsters spilt.

[b2.588] 3 [Alas, the cruel spear Went deep into his side, And the rich flood of purple gore Their murderous weapons dy'd.]

[b2.589] 4 [The waves of swelling grief Did o'er his bosom roll, And mountains of almighty wrath Lay heavy on his soul.]

[b2.590] 5 Down to the shades of death He bow'd his awful head, Yet he arose to live and reign When death itself is dead.

[b2.591] 6 No more the bloody spear, The cross and nails no more; For hell itself shakes at his Name, And all the heavens adore.

[b2.592] 7 There the Redeemer sits High on the Father's throne; The Father lays his vengeance by, And smiles upon his Son.

[b2.593] 8 There his full glories shine With uncreated rays, And bless his saints' and angels' eyes To everlasting days.

[b2.594] Hymn 2:85. Sufficiency of pardon.

[b2.595] 1 Why does your face, ye humble souls, Those mournful colours wear? What doubts are these that waste your faith, And nourish your despair?

[b2.596] 2 What tho' your numerous sins exceed The stars that fill the skies, And aiming at th' eternal throne, Like pointed mountains rise?

[b2.597] 3 What tho' your mighty guilt beyond The wide creation swell, And has its curs'd foundations laid Low as the deeps of hell?

[b2.598] 4 See here an endless ocean flows Of never-failing grace, Behold a dying Saviour's veins The sacred flood increase:

[b2.599] 5 It rises high and drowns the hills, 'T has neither shore nor bound: Nor if we search to find our sins, Our sins can ne'er be found.

[b2.600] 6 Awake, our hearts, adore the grace That buries all our faults, And pardoning blood that swells above Our follies and our thoughts.

[b2.601] Hymn 2:86. Freedom from sin and misery in heaven.

[b2.602] 1 Our sins, alas, how strong they be! And like a violent sea They break our duty, Lord, to thee, And hurry us away.

[b2.603] 2 The waves of trouble how they rise! How loud the tempests roar! But death shall land our weary souls Safe on the heavenly shore.

[b2.604] 3 There to fulfil his sweet commands Our speedy feet shall move, No sin shall clog our winged zeal, Or cool our burning love.

[b2.605] 4 There shall we sit, and sing, and tell The wonders of his grace, Till heavenly raptures fire our hearts, And smile in every face.

[b2.606] 5 For ever his dear sacred Name Shall dwell upon our tongue, And Jesus and salvation be The close of every song.

[b2.607] Hymn 2:87. The divine glories above our reason.

[b2.608] 1 How wond'rous great, how glorious bright Must our Creator be, Who dwells amidst the dazzling light Of vast infinity!

[b2.609] 2 Our soaring spirits upward rise Tow'rd the celestial throne, Fain would we see the blessed Three, And the Almighty One.

[b2.610] 3 Our reason stretches all its wings, And climbs above the skies; But still how far beneath thy feet Our groveling reason lies!

[b2.611] 4 [Lord, here we bend our humble souls, And awfully adore, For the weak pinions of our mind Can stretch a thought no more.]

[b2.612] 5 Thy glories infinitely rise Above our labouring tongue; In vain the highest seraph tries To form an equal song.

[b2.613] 6 [In humble notes our faith adores The great mysterious King, While angels strain their nobler powers, And sweep th' immortal string.]

[b2.614] Hymn 2:88. Salvation.

[b2.615] 1 Salvation! O, the joyful sound! 'Tis pleasure to our ears; A sovereign balm for every wound, A cordial for our fears.

[b2.616] 2 Buried in sorrow and in sin, At hell's dark door we lay, But we arise by grace divine To see a heavenly day.

[b2.617] 3 Salvation! let the echo fly The spacious earth around, While all the armies of the sky Conspire to raise the sound.

[b2.618] Hymn 2:89. Christ's victory over Satan.

[b2.619] 1 Hosanna to our conquering King! The prince of darkness flies, His troops rush headlong down to hell Like lightning from the skies.

[b2.620] 2 There, bound in chains, the lions roar, And fright the rescu'd sheep, But heavy bars confine their power And malice to the deep.

[b2.621] 3 Hosanna to our conquering King, All hail, incarnate love! Ten thousand songs and glories wait To crown thy head above.

[b2.622] 4 Thy victories and thy deathless fame Thro' the wide world shall run, And everlasting ages sing The triumphs thou hast won.

[b2.623] Hymn 2:90. Faith in Christ for pardon and sanctification.

[b2.624] 1 How sad our state by nature is! Our sin how deep it stains! And Satan binds our captive minds Fast in his slavish chains.

[b2.625] 2 But there's a voice of sovereign grace Sounds from the sacred word, "Ho, ye despairing sinners, come, "And trust upon the Lord."

[b2.626] 3 My soul obeys th' almighty call, And runs to this relief, I would believe thy promise, Lord, O! help my unbelief.

[b2.627] 4 [To the dear fountain of thy blood, Incarnate God, I fly, Here let me wash my spotted soul From crimes of deepest die.

[b2.628] 5 Stretch out thine arm victorious King, My reigning sins subdue, Drive the old dragon from his seat, With all his hellish crew.]

[b2.629] 6 A guilty, weak, and helpless worm, On thy kind arms I fall: Be thou my strength and righteousness, My Jesus, and my all.

[b2.630] Hymn 2:91. The glory of Christ in heaven.

[b2.631] 1 O the delights, the heavenly joys, The glories of the place Where Jesus sheds the brightest beams Of his o'erflowing grace.

[b2.632] 2 Sweet majesty and awful love Sit smiling on his brow, And all the glorious ranks above At humble distance bow.

[b2.633] 3 [Princes to his imperial Name Bend their bright sceptres down, Dominions, thrones, and powers rejoice To see him wear the crown.]

[b2.634] 4 Archangels sound his lofty praise Thro' every heavenly street, And lay their highest honours down Submissive at his feet.

[b2.635] 5 Those soft, those blessed feet of his That once rude iron tore, High on a throne of light they stand, And all the saints adore.

[b2.636] 6 His head, the dear majestic head That cruel thorns did wound, See what immortal glories shine, And circle it around.

[b2.637] 7 This is the Man, th' exalted Man, Whom we unseen adore; But when our eyes behold his face, Our hearts shall love him more.

[b2.638] 8 [Lord, how our souls are all on fire To see thy bless'd abode, Our tongues rejoice in tunes of praise To our incarnate God.

[b2.639] 9 And whilst our faith enjoys this sight, We long to leave our clay, And wish thy fiery chariots, Lord, To fetch our souls away.]

[b2.640] Hymn 2:92 The church saved, and her enemies disappointed.

[b2.641] Composed the 5th of November, 1694.

[b2.642] 1 Shout to the Lord, and let our joys Thro' the whole nation run; Ye British skies, resound the noise Beyond the rising sun.

[b2.643] 2 Thee, mighty God, our souls admire, Thee our glad voices sing, And join with the celestial choir To praise th' eternal King.

[b2.644] 3 Thy power the whole creation rules, And on the starry skies Sits smiling at the weak designs Thine envious foes devise.

[b2.645] 4 Thy scorn derides their feeble rage, And with an awful frown Flings vast confusion on their plots, And shakes their Babel down.

[b2.646] 5 [Their secret fires in caverns lay, And we the sacrifice: But gloomy caverns strove in vain To 'scape all-searching eyes.

[b2.647] 6 Their dark designs were all reveal'd, Their treasons all betray'd: Praise to the God that broke the snare Their cursed hands had laid.]

[b2.648] 7 In vain the busy sons of hell Still new rebellions try, Their souls shall pine with envious rage, And vex away and die.

[b2.649] 8 Almighty grace defends our land From their malicious power, Let Britain with united songs Almighty grace adore.

[b2.650] Hymn 2:93. God all, and in all, Psalm 73. 25.

[b2.651] 1 My God, my life, my love, To thee, to thee I call, I cannot live if thou remove, For thou art all in all.

[b2.652] 2 [Thy shining grace can cheer, This dungeon where I dwell; 'Tis paradise when thou art here; If thou depart, 'tis hell.]

[b2.653] 3 [The smilings of thy face, How amiable they are! 'Tis heaven to rest in thine embrace, And no where else but there.]

[b2.654] 4 [To thee, and thee alone, The angels owe their bliss; They sit around thy gracious throne, And dwell where Jesus is.]

[b2.655] 5 [Not all the harps above Can make a heavenly place, If God his residence remove, Or but conceal his face.]

[b2.656] 6 Nor earth nor all the sky Can one delight afford; No not a drop of real joy, Without thy presence, Lord.

[b2.657] 7 Thou art the sea of love, Where all my pleasures roll, The circle where my passions move, And centre of my soul.

[b2.658] 8 [To thee my spirits fly With infinite desire; And yet how far from thee I lie! Dear Jesus, raise me higher!]

[b2.659] Hymn 2:94. God my only happiness, Psalm 73. 25.

[b2.660] 1 My God, my portion, and my love, My everlasting all, I've none but thee in heaven above, Or on this earthly ball.

[b2.661] 2 [What empty things are all the skies, And this inferior clod! There's nothing here deserves my joys, There's nothing like my God.]

[b2.662] 3 [In vain the bright, the burning sun Scatters his feeble light; 'Tis thy sweet beams create my noon; If thou withdraw, 'tis night.

[b2.663] 4 And whilst upon my restless bed, Amongst the shades I roll, If my Redeemer shew his head 'Tis morning with my soul.]

[b2.664] 5 To thee we owe our wealth and friends, And health, and safe abode; Thanks to thy Name for meaner things, But they are not my God.

[b2.665] 6 How vain a toy is glittering wealth, If once compar'd to thee; Or what's my safety, or my health, Or all my friends to me?

[b2.666] 7 Were I possessor of the earth, And call'd the stars my own Without thy graces and thyself I were a wretch undone.

[b2.667] 8 Let others stretch their arms like seas, And grasp in all the shore, Grant me the visits of thy face, And I desire no more.

[b2.668] Hymn 2:95. Look on him whom they pierced, and mourn.

[b2.669] 1 Infinite grief! amazing woe! Behold my bleeding Lord: Hell and the Jews conspir'd his death, And us'd the Roman sword.

[b2.670] 2 O the sharp pangs of smarting pain My dear Redeemer bore, When knotty whips and ragged thorns His sacred body tore!

[b2.671] 3 But knotty whips and ragged thorns In vain do I accuse; In vain I blame the Roman bands, And the more spiteful Jews.

[b2.672] 4 'Twere you, my sins, my cruel sins, His chief tormentors were; Each of my crimes became a nail, And unbelief the spear.

[b2.673] 5 'Twere you that pull'd the vengeance down Upon his guiltless head: Break, break, my heart, O burst mine eyes! And let my sorrows bleed.

[b2.674] 6 Strike, mighty grace, my flinty soul, Till melting waters flow, And deep repentance drown mine eyes In undissembled woe.

[b2.675] Hymn 2:96. Distinguishing love; or, Angels punished and men saved.

[b2.676] 1 Down headlong from their native skies The rebel angels fell, And thunderbolts of flaming wrath Pursu'd them deep to hell.

[b2.677] 2 Down from the top of earthly bliss Rebellious man was hurl'd And Jesus stoop'd beneath the grave To reach a sinking world.

[b2.678] 3 O love of infinite degree! Unmeasurable grace! Must heaven's eternal Darling die To save a traitorous race?

[b2.679] 4 Must angels sink for ever down, And burn in quenchless fire, While God forsakes his shining throne To raise us wretches higher?

[b2.680] 5 O for this love let earth and skies With hallelujahs ring, And the full choir of human tongues All hallelujah sing.

[b2.681] Hymn 2:97. The same.

[b2.682] 1 From heaven the sinning angels fell, And wrath and darkness chain'd them down; But man, vile man, forsook his bliss, And mercy lifts him to a crown.

[b2.683] 2 Amazing work of sovereign grace That could distinguish rebels so! Our guilty treasons call'd aloud For everlasting fetters too.

[b2.684] 3 To thee, to thee, Almighty Love, Our souls, ourselves, our all we pay: Millions of tongues shall sound thy praise On the bright hills of heavenly day.

[b2.685] Hymn 2:98. Hardness of heart complained of.

[b2.686] 1 My heart, how dreadful hard it is! How heavy here it lies, Heavy and cold within my breast Just like a rock of ice!

[b2.687] 2 Sin like a raging tyrant sits Upon this flinty throne, And every grace lies buried deep Beneath this heart of stone.

[b2.688] 3 How seldom do I rise to God, Or taste the joys above! This mountain presses down my faith, And chills my flaming love.

[b2.689] 4 When smiling mercy courts my soul With all its heavenly charms, This stubborn, this relentless thing Would thrust it from my arms.

[b2.690] 5 Against the thunders of thy word Rebellious I have stood, My heart it shakes not at the wrath And terrors of a God.

[b2.691] 6 Dear Saviour, steep this rock of mine In thine own crimson sea: None but a bath of blood divine Can melt the flint away.

[b2.692] Hymn 2:99. The book of God's decrees.

[b2.693] 1 Let the whole race of creatures lie Abas'd before their God; Whate'er his sovereign voice hath form'd He governs with a nod.

[b2.694] 2 [Ten thousand ages ere the skies Were into motion brought, All the long years and worlds to come Stood present to his thought.]

[b2.695] 5 [There's not a sparrow or a worm But's found in his decrees; He raises monarchs to their thrones, And sinks them as he please.]

[b2.696] 4 If light attends the course I run, 'Tis he provides those rays; And 'tis his hand that hides my sun, If darkness cloud my days.

[b2.697] 5 Yet I would not be much concern'd, Nor vainly long to see The volume of his deep decrees, What months are writ for me.

[b2.698] 6 When he reveals the book of life, O, may I read my name Amongst the chosen of his love, The followers of the Lamb!

[b2.699] Hymn 2:100. The presence of Christ is the life of my soul.

[b2.700] 1 [How full of anguish is the thought, How it distracts and tears my heart If God, at last, my sovereign Judge, Should frown, and bid my soul, "Depart!"]

[b2.701] 2 Lord, when I quit this earthly stage, Where shall I fly but to thy breast? For I have sought no other home For I have learn'd no other rest.

[b2.702] 3 I cannot live contented here, Without some glimpses of thy face; And heaven without thy presence there Would be a dark and tiresome place.

[b2.703] 4 When earthly cares engross the day And hold my thoughts aside from thee, The shining hours of cheerful light Are long and tedious years to me.

[b2.704] 5 And if no evening visit's paid Between my Saviour and my soul, How dull the night! how sad the shade! How mournfully the minutes roll!

[b2.705] 6 This flesh of mine might learn as soon To live, yet part with all my blood; To breathe when vital air is gone, Or thrive and grow without my food.

[b2.706] 7 [Christ is my light, my life, my care, My blessed hope, my heavenly prize, Dearer than all my passions are, My limbs, my bowels, or my eyes.

[b2.707] 8 The strings that twine about my heart, Tortures and racks may tear them off, But they can never, never part With their dear hold of Christ my love.]

[b2.708] 9 [My God! and can an humble child That loves thee with a flame so high, Be ever from thy face exil'd Without the pity of thine eye?

[b2.709] 10 Impossible--For thine own hands Have tied my heart so fast to thee; And in thy book the promise stands, That where thou art thy friends must be.

[b2.710] Hymn 2:101. The world's three great temptations.

[b2.711] 1 When in the light of faith divine We look on things below, Honour, and gold, and sensual joy, How vain and dangerous too!

[b2.712] 2 [Honour's a puff of noisy breath; Yet men expose their blood, And venture everlasting death To gain that airy good.

[b2.713] 3 Whilst others starve the nobler mind, And feed on shining dust, They rob the serpent of his food T' indulge a sordid lust.]

[b2.714] 4 The pleasures that allure our sense Are dangerous snares to souls; There's but a drop of flattering sweet, And dash'd with bitter bowls.

[b2.715] 5 God is mine all-sufficient good, My portion and my choice; In him my vast desires are fill'd, And all my powers rejoice.

[b2.716] 6 In vain the world accosts my ear, And tempts my heart anew; I cannot buy your bliss so dear, Nor part with heaven for you.

[b2.717] Hymn 2:102. A happy resurrection.

[b2.718] 1 No, I'll repine at death no more, But with a cheerful gasp resign To the cold dungeon of the grave These dying, withering limbs of mine.

[b2.719] 2 Let worms devour my wasting flesh, And crumble all my bones to dust, My God shall raise my frame anew At the revival of the just.

[b2.720] 3 Break, sacred morning, thro' the skies, Bring that delightful, dreadful day, Cut short the hours, dear Lord, and come, Thy lingering wheels, how long they stay!

[b2.721] 4 [Our weary spirits faint to see The light of thy returning face, And hear the language of those lips Where God has shed his richest grace.]

[b2.722] 5 Haste then upon the wings of love, Rouse all the pious sleeping clay, That we may join in heavenly joys, And sing the triumph of the day.

[b2.723] Hymn 2:103. Christ's commission, John 3. 16 17.

[b2.724] 1 Come happy souls, approach your God With new melodious songs; Come, render to almighty grace The tribute of your tongues.

[b2.725] 2 So strange, so boundless was the love That pity'd dying men, The Father sent his equal Son To give them life again.

[b2.726] 3 Thy hands, dear Jesus, were not arm'd With a revenging rod, No hard commission to perform The vengeance of a God.

[b2.727] 4 But all was mercy, all was mild, And wrath forsook the throne, When Christ on the kind errand came, And brought salvation down.

[b2.728] 5 Here, sinners, you may heal your wounds, And wipe your sorrows dry; Trust in the mighty Saviour's Name, And you shall never die.

[b2.729] 6 See, dearest Lord our willing souls Accept thine offer'd grace; We bless the great Redeemer's love, And give the Father praise.

[b2.730] Hymn 2:104. The same.

[b2.731] 1 Raise your triumphant songs To an immortal tune, Let the wide earth resound the deeds Celestial grace has done.

[b2.732] 2 Sing how eternal love Its chief beloved chose, And bid him raise our wretched race From their abyss of woes.

[b2.733] 3 His hand no thunder bears, Nor terror clothes his brow, No bolts to drive our guilty souls To fiercer flames below.

[b2.734] 4 'Twas mercy fill'd the throne, And wrath stood silent by, When Christ was sent with pardons down To rebels doom'd to die.

[b2.735] 5 Now, sinners, dry your tears, Let hopeless sorrow cease; Bow to the sceptre of his love, And take the offer'd peace.

[b2.736] 6 Lord we obey thy call; We lay an humble claim To the salvation thou hast brought, And love and praise thy Name.

[b2.737] Hymn 2:105. Repentance flowing from the patience of God.

[b2.738] 1 And are we wretches yet alive? And do we yet rebel? 'Tis boundless, 'tis amazing love That bears us up from hell!

[b2.739] 2 The burden of our weighty guilt Would sink us down to flames, And threatening vengeance rolls above To crush our feeble frames.

[b2.740] 3 Almighty goodness cries, "Forbear;" And straight the thunder stays; And dare we now provoke his wrath, And weary out his grace?

[b2.741] 4 Lord, we have long abus'd thy love, Too long indulg'd our sin; Our aching hearts e'en bleed to see What rebels we have been.

[b2.742] 5 No more, ye lusts, shall ye command, No more will we obey; Stretch out, O God, thy conquering hand, And drive thy foes away.

[b2.743] Hymn 2:106. Repentance at the cross.

[b2.744] 1 Oh, if my soul was form'd for woe, How would I vent my sighs! Repentance should like rivers flow From both my streaming eyes.

[b2.745] 2 'Twas for my sins, my dearest Lord Hung on the cursed tree, And groan'd away a dying life, For thee, my soul, for thee.

[b2.746] 3 O how I hate those lusts of mine That crucify'd my God, Those sins that pierc'd and nail'd his flesh Fast to the fatal wood!

[b2.747] 4 Yes, my Redeemer, they shall die, My heart has so decreed, Nor will I spare the guilty things That made my Saviour bleed.

[b2.748] 5 Whilst with a melting broken heart My murder'd Lord I view, I'll raise revenge against my sins, And slay the murderers too.

[b2.749] Hymn 2:107. The everlasting absence of God intolerable.

[b2.750] 1 That awful day will surely come, Th' appointed hour makes haste, When I must stand before my Judge, And pass the solemn test.

[b2.751] 2 Thou lovely chief of all my joys, Thou sovereign of my heart, How could I bear to hear thy voice Pronounce the sound, "Depart?"

[b2.752] 3 [The thunder of that dismal word, Would so torment my ear, 'Twould tear my soul asunder, Lord, With most tormenting fear.]

[b2.753] 4 [What, to be banish'd from my life, And yet forbid to die! To linger in eternal pain, Yet death for ever fly!]

[b2.754] 5 O wretched state of deep despair, To see my God remove, And fix my doleful station where I must not taste his Love.

[b2.755] 6 Jesus, I throw my arms around, And hang upon thy breast; Without a gracious smile from thee My spirit cannot rest.

[b2.756] 7 O tell me that my worthless name Is graven on thy hands; Shew me some promise in thy book Where my salvation stands!

[b2.757] 8 [Give me one kind assuring word, To sink my fears again, And cheerfully my soul shall wait Her threescore years and ten.]

[b2.758] Hymn 2:108. Access to the throne of grace by a Mediator.

[b2.759] 1 Come, let us lift our joyful eyes Up to the courts above, And smile to see our Father there Upon a throne of love.

[b2.760] 2 Once 'twas a seat of dreadful wrath, And shot devouring flame; Our God appear'd consuming fire, And vengeance was his name.

[b2.761] 3 Rich were the drops of Jesus' blood, That calm'd his frowning face, That sprinkled o'er the burning throne, And turn'd the wrath to grace.

[b2.762] 4 Now we may bow before his feet, And venture near the Lord; No fiery cherub guards his seat, Nor double-flaming sword.

[b2.763] 5 The peaceful gates of heavenly bliss Are open'd by the Son; High let us raise our notes of praise, And reach th' Almighty throne.

[b2.764] 6 To thee ten thousand thanks we bring Great Advocate on high: And glory to th' eternal King That lays his fury by.

[b2.765] Hymn 2:109. The darkness of providence.

[b2.766] 1 Lord, we adore thy vast designs, Th' obscure abyss of providence, Too deep to sound with mortal lines Too dark to view with feeble sense.

[b2.767] 2 Now thou array'st thine awful face In angry frowns, without a smile; We thro' the cloud believe thy grace, Secure of thy compassion still.

[b2.768] 3 Thro' seas and storms of deep distress We sail by faith and not by sight; Faith guides us in the wilderness Through all the briers and the night.

[b2.769] 4 Dear Father, if thy lifted rod Resolve to scourge us here below, Still we must lean upon our God, Thine arm shall bear us safely thro'.

[b2.770] Hymn 2:110. Triumph over death in hope of the resurrection.

[b2.771] 1 And must this body die? This mortal frame decay? And must these active limbs of mine Lie mouldering in the clay?

[b2.772] 2 Corruption, earth and worms, Shall but refine this flesh, Till my triumphant spirit comes To put it on afresh.

[b2.773] 3 God my Redeemer lives, And often from the skies Looks down, and watches all my dust, Till he shall bid it rise.

[b2.774] 4 Array'd in glorious grace Shall these vile bodies shine, And every shape and every face Look heavenly and divine.

[b2.775] 5 These lively hopes we owe To Jesus' dying love; We would adore his grace below, And sing his power above.

[b2.776] 6 Dear Lord, accept the praise Of these our humble songs, Till tunes of nobler sound we raise With our immortal tongues.

[b2.777] Hymn 2:111. Thanksgiving for victory; or, God's dominion and our deliverance.

[b2.778] 1 Zion rejoice, and Judah sing; The Lord assumes his throne; Let Britain own the heavenly King, And make his glories known.

[b2.779] 2 The great, the wicked, and the proud, From their high seats are hurl'd; Jehovah rides upon a cloud, And thunders thro' the world.

[b2.780] 3 He reigns upon th' eternal hills, Distributes mortal crowns, Empires are fix'd beneath his smiles, And totter at his frowns.

[b2.781] 4 Navies, that rule the ocean wide, Are vanquish'd by his breath; And legions arm'd with power and pride Descend to watery death.

[b2.782] 5 Let tyrants make no more pretence To vex our happy land; Jehovah's Name is our defence, Our buckler is his hand.

[b2.783] 6 [Long may the King our Sovereign live To rule us by his word; And all the honours he can give Be offer'd to the Lord.]

[b2.784] Hymn 2:112. Angels ministering to Christ and saints.

[b2.785] 1 Great God, to what a glorious height Hast thou advanc'd the Lord thy Son! Angels, in all their robes of light, Are made the servants of his throne.

[b2.786] 2 Before his feet their armies wait, And swift as flames of fire they move, To manage his affairs of state In works of vengeance or of love.

[b2.787] 3 His orders run thro' all their hosts, Legions descend at his command To shield and guard the British coasts, When foreign rage invades our land.

[b2.788] 4 Now they are sent to guide our feet Up to the gates of thine abode, Thro' all the dangers that we meet In travelling the heavenly road.

[b2.789] 5 Lord, when I leave this mortal ground, And thou shall bid me rise and come, Send a beloved angel down Safe to conduct my spirit home.

[b2.790] Hymn 2:113. The same.

[b2.791] 1 The majesty of Solomon! How glorious to behold The servants waiting round his throne, The ivory and the gold.

[b2.792] 2 But, mighty God, thy palace shines With far superior beams; Thine angel-guards are swift as winds, Thy ministers are flames.

[b2.793] 3 [Soon as thine only Son had made His entrance on this earth, A shining army downward fled To celebrate his birth.

[b2.794] 4 And when oppress'd with pains and fears On the cold ground he lies, Behold a heavenly form appears T' allay his agonies.]

[b2.795] 5 Now to the hands of Christ our King Are all their legions given; They wait upon his saints, and bring His chosen heirs to heaven.

[b2.796] 6 Pleasure and praise run thro' their host To see a sinner turn; Then Satan has a captive lost, And Christ a subject born.

[b2.797] 7 But there's an hour of brighter joy, When he his angels sends Obstinate rebels to destroy, And gather in his friends.

[b2.798] 8 O! could I say, without a doubt, There shall my soul be found, Then let the great archangel shout, And the last trumpet sound.

[b2.799] Hymn 2:114. Christ's death, victory and dominion.

[b2.800] 1 I sing my Saviour's wondrous death; He conquer'd when he fell: 'Tis finish'd, said his dying breath, And shook the gates of hell.

[b2.801] 2 'Tis finish'd, our Immanuel cries, The dreadful work is done; Hence shall his sovereign throne arise, His kingdom is begun.

[b2.802] 3 His cross a sure foundation laid For glory and renown, When thro' the regions of the dead He pass'd to reach the crown.

[b2.803] 4 Exalted at his Father's side Sits our victorious Lord; To heaven and hell his hands divide The vengeance or reward.

[b2.804] 5 The saints from his propitious eye Await their several crowns, And all the sons of darkness fly The terror of his frowns.

[b2.805] Hymn 2:115. God the avenger of his saints; or, His kingdom.

[b2.806] 1 High as the heavens above the ground Reigns the Creator God; Wide as the whole creation's bound Extends his awful rod.

[b2.807] 2 Let princes of exalted state To him ascribe their crown, Render their homage at his feet, And cast their glories down.

[b2.808] 3 Know that his kingdom is supreme, Your lofty thoughts are vain; He calls you gods, that awful name, But ye must die like men.

[b2.809] 4 Then let the sovereigns of the globe Not dare to vex the just; He puts on vengeance like a robe, And treads the worms to dust.

[b2.810] 5 Ye judges of the earth, be wise, And think on heaven with fear; The meanest saint that you despise Has an avenger there.

[b2.811] Hymn 2:116. Mercies and thanks.

[b2.812] 1 How can I sink with such a prop As my eternal God, Who bears the earth's huge pillars up, And spreads the heavens abroad?

[b2.813] 2 How can I die while Jesus lives, Who rose and left the dead? Pardon and grace my soul receives From mine exalted head.
